Test case (id ba9c7920-3701-495e-8fbf-4e9bf3e28f44, title 'qa-chaos-dup-ioc-case') and both duplicate rows were deleted afterward (case_iocs then cases, FK-safe order) and confirmed removed.
- **Reproduction:** POST /api/v1/cases/{case_id}/iocs twice, near-simultaneously, with identical {ioc_value, ioc_type} body. Both succeed (201); GET the case afterward and count matching ioc_value entries -- 2 instead of 1.

#### 47. Logging out in one tab leaves a second tab of the same account looking fully signed-in, with no live warning and no forced re-login on the next action

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Frontend chaos testing (real browser automation)
- **Title:** Logging out in one tab leaves a second tab of the same account looking fully signed-in, with no live warning and no forced re-login on the next action
- **Description:** Auth state lives in `localStorage` (`access_token`/`refresh_token`), which is shared synchronously across all tabs of the same browser profile/origin, but there is no `storage` event listener anywhere in the frontend to detect a cross-tab logout. After tab A signs out (clearing both tokens), tab B -- which was mid-investigation -- keeps rendering the completed investigation UI indefinitely (basket badge count, action buttons, etc. all still shown as if logged in) because nothing re-checks auth state. Its in-flight SSE stream finishes normally regardless (the JWT was already sent in the original request header). The FIRST subsequent authenticated action in tab B (e.g. 'Add to Basket') does not redirect to /login: `authedFetch()`'s 401-then-failed-refresh path (lib/api.ts) has no onAuthExpired-style hook -- that redirect behavior exists only for the dedicated SSE lookup-stream path (app/lookup/new/page.tsx's onAuthExpired). The user just sees the same generic 'Failed to add to basket.' message used for any other transient error, with no indication that they need to sign in again.
- **Evidence:** Screenshot chaos-21-5-tabB-add-to-basket-after-session-died.png (C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\documentation\build\raw-screenshots\) shows tab B still fully rendered (Basket badge '3', all panels populated, verdict shown) with 'Failed to add to basket.' printed under Investigation Actions -- no login prompt, no banner, URL unchanged (still on /lookup/new?value=chaos-logoutrace-...), tabBUrlNow.includes('/login') === false.
- **Reproduction:** Tab A and Tab B both logged in as the same account (shared localStorage). Start an investigation in tab B. In tab A, click 'Sign out'. Wait for tab B's investigation to finish, then click 'Add to Basket' in tab B: no redirect to /login occurs; a generic failure message appears instead.

#### 53. @qa.test disposable email domain is rejected by /auth/login and /auth/register with HTTP 422, contrary to the stated QA convention

- **Severity:** P4
- **Dimension:** Audit log integrity
- **Title:** @qa.test disposable email domain is rejected by /auth/login and /auth/register with HTTP 422, contrary to the stated QA convention
- **Description:** The LoginRequest/RegisterRequest pydantic schemas use EmailStr (via the email-validator library), which rejects the .test TLD as a 'special-use or reserved name' with a 422 before the request ever reaches application logic or the database. Confirmed: POST /auth/login with email ending in @qa.test or @test.qa.test returns 422 'value is not a valid email address: The part after the @-sign is a special-use or reserved name...', while the identical request with @example.com or @qa.example.com returns the expected 401 for a nonexistent user. This means personas following the '@qa.test' half of the stated disposable-data convention cannot actually authenticate through the real HTTP API at all (only bypassable via direct service-layer/DB calls, which never exercises the real login code path or its auth.login/auth.login_failed audit entries). Not an audit-log-integrity bug itself, but it would silently break any tester's login/failed-login audit test if they picked the .test suffix, so I switched my login-capable personas to @example.com after discovering this.
- **Evidence:** docker exec app-backend-1 python3 httpx POST /api/v1/auth/login {"email":"nonexistent@qa.test",...} -> 422 {"detail":[{"type":"value_error","loc":["body","email"],"msg":"value is not a valid email address: The part after the @-sign is a special-use or reserved name that cannot be used with email.",...}]}; same call with @example.com -> 401 {"detail":"Invalid email or password"}.
- **Reproduction:** —
- **category:** test-environment-gotcha
- **failure_scenario:** A tester creates persona 'someone@qa.test', calls POST /api/v1/auth/login with correct credentials expecting a 200 + auth.login audit row, and instead gets a 422 from pydantic email validation with no audit entry at all (correctly so, since the request never reached the login handler) -- easy to misread as an audit-logging gap rather than an email-validation quirk.

#### 54. Weak, hardcoded default datastore credentials in source are actually in effect on the live Postgres container

- **Severity:** P2
- **Dimension:** Independent secret scan (dev tree: C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform)
- **Title:** Weak, hardcoded default datastore credentials in source are actually in effect on the live Postgres container
- **Description:** backend/app/core/config.py:39 hardcodes `database_url: str = "postgresql+asyncpg://ioc:ioc@postgres:5432/ioc_intel"` and :43 hardcodes `neo4j_password: str = "changeme-neo4j"`; docker-compose.yml mirrors this with `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-ioc}` (lines 6, 76, 115) and `NEO4J_AUTH: neo4j/${NEO4J_PASSWORD:-changeme-neo4j}` (line 41). The dev-tree .env does not set POSTGRES_PASSWORD, NEO4J_PASSWORD, POSTGRES_USER, or POSTGRES_DB at all (confirmed via grep -c presence-count, 0 matches for each), so any deployment that skips the Windows setup wizard's random-secret generation (windows/scripts/Write-EnvFile.ps1's `New-RandomSecret -Bytes 24` for PostgresPassword/Neo4jPassword) and just runs `docker compose up` silently falls back to these guessable defaults. Verified live: querying the running app-backend-1 container's actual `Settings()` object shows `database_url` still equals the literal default containing `ioc:ioc@` (boolean check returned True), i.e. the live Postgres instance backing this threat-intel platform is currently reachable with the well-known default password baked into committed source/compose files. (Neo4j's live password was separately confirmed NOT to be the default, so this specific weakness is Postgres-only in the current running instance.)
- **Evidence:** backend/app/core/config.py:39 `database_url: str = "postgresql+asyncpg://ioc:ioc@postgres:5432/ioc_intel"`; docker-compose.yml:6 `POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-ioc}`; dev-tree .env has 0 lines matching `^POSTGRES_PASSWORD=`; live check `docker exec app-backend-1 python -c "from app.core.config import Settings; print('ioc:ioc@' in Settings().database_url)"` returned True.
- **Reproduction:** 1) Inspect backend/app/core/config.py line 39 and docker-compose.yml lines 6/41/76/115. 2) `grep -c '^POSTGRES_PASSWORD=' .env` in the dev tree returns 0, confirming no override. 3) Run the boolean check above against app-backend-1 to confirm the default is the value actually in effect.

#### 55. Insecure hardcoded fallback JWT signing secret ships in source (fail-open on missing config)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Independent secret scan (dev tree: C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform)
- **Title:** Insecure hardcoded fallback JWT signing secret ships in source (fail-open on missing config)
- **Description:** backend/app/core/config.py:29 hardcodes `jwt_secret_key: str = Field(default="change-me-in-production")`. If JWT_SECRET_KEY is ever absent from the environment (e.g. a misconfigured deployment, a stripped-down .env, or a container started without its env_file), the app starts successfully anyway and silently signs/verifies all auth JWTs with this exact, publicly-committed string -- letting anyone who has read this source file forge a valid access token for any user/role via `create_access_token` semantics, with no startup warning or crash. Currently NOT the active value on either the dev-tree .env or the live container (both confirmed non-default, non-empty), so this is a latent/defense-in-depth gap rather than an active compromise today.
- **Evidence:** backend/app/core/config.py:29 `jwt_secret_key: str = Field(default="change-me-in-production")`. Live check `docker exec app-backend-1 python -c "from app.core.config import Settings; print(Settings().jwt_secret_key == 'change-me-in-production')"` returned False (not currently exploited).
- **Reproduction:** Inspect backend/app/core/config.py line 29. To reproduce impact: unset JWT_SECRET_KEY in the environment and start the backend -- it will boot without error and accept the hardcoded default as its signing key instead of refusing to start.

#### 56. Hardcoded plaintext admin/analyst passwords committed in documentation walkthrough scripts

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Independent secret scan (dev tree: C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform)
- **Title:** Hardcoded plaintext admin/analyst passwords committed in documentation walkthrough scripts
- **Description:** documentation/build/walkthrough.js:16-17 hardcodes `ADMIN_EMAIL = 'final-admin@example.com'` / `ADMIN_PASSWORD = 'FinalTestPass123!'`; documentation/build/walkthrough-admin.js:14-15 hardcodes `ADMIN_EMAIL = 'docs-walkthrough-admin@example.com'` / `ADMIN_PASSWORD = 'WalkthroughQA-2026!'`; documentation/build/walkthrough-secassess.js:13-14 hardcodes `ANALYST_EMAIL = 'secassess-walkthrough@example.com'` / `ANALYST_PASSWORD = 'WalkthroughQA-2026!'`. These scripts only type the credentials into the live login form (they do not self-register), meaning the accounts were manually created against the real running app at some point with these exact plaintext passwords. None of the three emails currently exist in the users table (checked via psql, 18 total users, none matching), so there is no immediately exploitable live account right now, but the plaintext passwords remain permanently readable in the dev tree and would become live credentials again the moment any of these accounts is recreated with a matching password (e.g. for a future documentation refresh).
- **Evidence:** documentation/build/walkthrough.js:17 `const ADMIN_PASSWORD = 'FinalTestPass123!';`; documentation/build/walkthrough-admin.js:15 and walkthrough-secassess.js:14 both `const ...PASSWORD = 'WalkthroughQA-2026!';`. `docker exec app-postgres-1 psql -U ioc -d ioc_intel -c "SELECT email,role FROM users;"` lists 18 users, none matching final-admin@example.com / docs-walkthrough-admin@example.com / secassess-walkthrough@example.com.
- **Reproduction:** Read the three files at the line numbers above. Cross-check current DB state with `docker exec app-postgres-1 psql -U ioc -d ioc_intel -c "SELECT email FROM users;"` to confirm no matching live account exists today.

- **Title:** Dev-tree .env contains real-looking third-party API keys and an AWS key pair in plaintext (correctly gitignored, not the config the live containers actually load)
- **Description:** C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\.env has non-empty values (lengths only checked, no values printed) for JWT_SECRET_KEY (33 chars), AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID (20 chars, matches AKIA-prefixed real AWS access-key-ID format per grep), AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY (40 chars, matches real AWS secret-key length), GEMINI_API_KEY (53 chars), VIRUSTOTAL_API_KEY (64 chars), ABUSEIPDB_API_KEY (80 chars), OTX_API_KEY (64 chars), NVD_API_KEY (36 chars, UUID-shaped), ABUSECH_AUTH_KEY (48 chars), HYBRID_ANALYSIS_API_KEY (64 chars), and CENSYS_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN (40 chars) -- all lengths consistent with genuine (non-placeholder) credentials for those services, versus .env.example where every one of these keys is correctly left blank. This is expected local developer configuration, not a defect: .gitignore:1-2 excludes `.env` and `.env.*` (with `.env.example` explicitly un-ignored), the directory has no .git history to leak through, and per the memory note on this platform's dual source tree, this dev-tree .env is not even the file the running Docker containers actually load (confirmed indirectly: the live backend's JWT secret length, 61 chars, does not match this file's 33 chars, so the installed copy under Program Files has its own separate .env). Flagged for completeness per the task's instruction to check .env for real-looking values, not as a vulnerability.
- **Evidence:** awk-based length-only scan of ioc-intel-platform\.env (no values printed) vs. ioc-intel-platform\.env.example (every corresponding key blank); .gitignore lines 1-2 (`.env` / `.env.*` / `!.env.example`); grep for `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{12,}` matched only this file.
- **Reproduction:** Compare key-by-key: `.env.example` has every provider key blank; `.env` has non-empty, format-plausible values of the lengths listed above. `.gitignore` confirms `.env` is excluded from version control.

#### 58. frontend package.json pins next==14.2.15, which OSV.dev lists 30 confirmed advisories against for this exact version, including a critical (CVSS 9.1) middleware auth-bypass

- **Severity:** P2
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** frontend package.json pins next==14.2.15, which OSV.dev lists 30 confirmed advisories against for this exact version, including a critical (CVSS 9.1) middleware auth-bypass
- **Description:** Direct query to https://api.osv.dev/v1/query for package 'next' ecosystem 'npm' version '14.2.15' returned 30 vulns, including GHSA-f82v-jwr5-mffw/CVE-2025-29927 'Authorization Bypass in Next.js Middleware' (fixed in 14.2.25) and clusters of SSRF, HTTP request smuggling, XSS, and cache-poisoning advisories in Server Actions, Image Optimization, and rewrites, most dated through 2026. `npm audit --omit=dev` in frontend/ independently flagged the same `next` range as critical. I checked which attack surfaces this specific app actually uses: `find` for middleware.ts/tsx under frontend/ found none (only vendored copies inside node_modules/.next), next.config.js has no rewrites/i18n (just reactStrictMode + output:standalone), and `grep` for '"use server"'/'next/image'/'remotePatterns' under frontend/app and frontend/components found zero matches. That rules out the middleware-bypass, rewrites-SSRF/smuggling, i18n-middleware-bypass, Server-Actions, and Image-Optimization advisory clusters specifically for this deployment. What's NOT ruled out: a residual cluster of React-Server-Component cache-poisoning/confusion advisories (e.g. GHSA-vfv6-92ff-j949, GHSA-wfc6-r584-vfw7, GHSA-4633-3j49-mh5q) that are inherent to App Router's RSC machinery regardless of whether the app writes its own Server Actions, since App Router uses RSC for every route by default. Next.js is the actual internet/LAN-facing web tier for this app, ships frequent patch-only fixes, and 14.2.15 is now nearly a year of patch releases behind 14.2.x HEAD -- this is the single highest-value, lowest-risk version bump in the whole inventory (patch release, not a major bump).
- **Evidence:** OSV query from app-backend-1: POST https://api.osv.dev/v1/query {package:{name:'next',ecosystem:'npm'},version:'14.2.15'} -> 30 vulns incl. GHSA-f82v-jwr5-mffw (CVE-2025-29927, fixed 14.2.25). `npm audit --omit=dev` in frontend/ flags `next 0.9.9 - 16.3.0-preview.10` critical. `find frontend -iname 'middleware.*'` returns only node_modules/.next paths, no app-owned middleware file. grep for '"use server"|next/image|remotePatterns' under frontend/app and frontend/components: no files found.
- **Reproduction:** docker exec app-frontend-1 (or host) npm audit --omit=dev inside C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\frontend; or POST {"package":{"name":"next","ecosystem":"npm"},"version":"14.2.15"} to https://api.osv.dev/v1/query

#### 59. backend/requirements.txt pins cryptography==43.0.1, which OSV.dev lists 11 advisories against, several in certificate-validation logic that this app's TLS scanner and credential-encryption code paths both depend on

- **Severity:** P2
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** backend/requirements.txt pins cryptography==43.0.1, which OSV.dev lists 11 advisories against, several in certificate-validation logic that this app's TLS scanner and credential-encryption code paths both depend on
- **Description:** Direct OSV query for 'cryptography' 43.0.1 returned 11 vulns: two 'vulnerable OpenSSL bundled in cryptography wheels' advisories (GHSA-537c-gmf6-5ccf, CVE-2024-12797/GHSA-79v4-65xg-pq4g), plus four 2026-dated cert/crypto-validation issues -- CVE-2026-69248 'verifier accepts wildcard DNS names allowing escape from permittedSubtrees', CVE-2026-34073 'incomplete DNS name constraint enforcement on peer names', CVE-2026-69249 'duplicate self-signed intermediates can cause exponential path-building' (DoS), and CVE-2026-26007 'missing subgroup validation for SECT curves' (signature-forgery-class weakness). This package is not incidental here: grep confirms backend/app/core/crypto.py imports `cryptography.fernet.Fernet` (referenced from backend/app/models/runtime_config.py, consistent with the platform's stored-provider-API-key encryption-at-rest), and backend/app/security_assessment/tls_tool.py imports from `cryptography` for its TLS/certificate assessment tool -- i.e. the two places this library is used are (a) protecting secrets at rest and (b) parsing/validating X.509 certificates presented by scan targets, which is exactly the code path the DNS-name-constraint and wildcard-escape CVEs affect. I did not build a PoC exploiting these specific CVEs against tls_tool.py (out of scope for a dependency-inventory pass), so treat this as a verified version-currency/exposure finding, not a demonstrated exploit.
- **Evidence:** OSV query from app-backend-1 for cryptography==43.0.1 returned 11 vulns including GHSA-m2h6-j472-rp4c/CVE-2026-69248, GHSA-m959-cc7f-wv43/CVE-2026-34073, GHSA-jwv3-5hgf-82ww/CVE-2026-69249, GHSA-r6ph-v2qm-q3c2/CVE-2026-26007. grep -rln 'Fernet|from cryptography' backend/app found backend/app/core/crypto.py, backend/app/models/runtime_config.py, backend/app/security_assessment/tls_tool.py, backend/app/tests/unit/test_crypto.py.
- **Reproduction:** docker exec app-backend-1 python -c "import urllib.request,json; print(urllib.request.urlopen(urllib.request.Request('https://api.osv.dev/v1/query', data=json.dumps({'package':{'name':'cryptography','ecosystem':'PyPI'},'version':'43.0.1'}).encode(), headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})).read())"

#### 60. fastapi==0.115.0 pulls in starlette 0.38.6 (unpinned, transitive), which OSV.dev lists 14 advisories against, though this app's own routes don't touch the specifically-affected features

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** fastapi==0.115.0 pulls in starlette 0.38.6 (unpinned, transitive), which OSV.dev lists 14 advisories against, though this app's own routes don't touch the specifically-affected features
- **Description:** starlette is not listed directly in requirements.txt -- it resolves transitively via fastapi==0.115.0's dependency spec to 0.38.6 (confirmed via `pip list` in app-backend-1). Direct OSV query for starlette 0.38.6 returned 14 vulns spanning 2024-2026: multipart/form-data DoS (CVE-2024-47874, CVE-2025-54121), urlencoded-form DoS (CVE-2026-54283), Host-header/path poisoning bypassing path-based security checks (CVE-2026-48710, CVE-2026-54282), StaticFiles SSRF/NTLM-credential-theft via UNC paths on Windows (CVE-2026-48818), and arbitrary HTTP-method dispatch via HTTPEndpoint getattr (CVE-2026-48817). I checked applicability: grep for UploadFile/Form(/multipart across backend/app found zero matches in app-owned code (only inside vendored .venv site-packages) and the login route uses a JSON body, not OAuth2PasswordRequestForm -- so the multipart/urlencoded-form DoS advisories aren't reachable through any current endpoint. grep for StaticFiles/HTTPEndpoint/request.url.hostname/Host-header trust across backend/app also found zero matches -- the app doesn't use Starlette's StaticFiles or class-based HTTPEndpoint, and doesn't appear to trust Host header for security decisions. Also, app-backend-1 runs Linux containers in production, so the Windows-only StaticFiles/UNC-path CVE is moot for the actual deployment regardless. Net: real exploitability today looks low because the vulnerable code paths aren't exercised, but starlette isn't pinned directly, so a routine `pip install -U fastapi` later will silently change it -- worth pinning starlette explicitly or upgrading fastapi (which will pull a patched starlette) as routine hygiene rather than an emergency.
- **Evidence:** docker exec app-backend-1 pip list shows starlette 0.38.6 though it's absent from requirements.txt. OSV query for starlette==0.38.6 (npm ecosystem PyPI) returned 14 vulns incl. GHSA-86qp-5c8j-p5mr/CVE-2026-48710, GHSA-wqp7-x3pw-xc5r/CVE-2026-48818, GHSA-x746-7m8f-x49c/CVE-2026-48817. grep -rln 'UploadFile|multipart' backend/app (excluding .venv) returned no app-owned files; grep for 'StaticFiles|HTTPEndpoint|request.url.hostname' returned no files.
- **Reproduction:** docker exec app-backend-1 pip show starlette; then query https://api.osv.dev/v1/query with {package:{name:'starlette',ecosystem:'PyPI'},version:'0.38.6'}

#### 61. python-jose==3.3.0 carries a critical (CVSS 9.3) algorithm-confusion CVE and a JWT/JWE-bomb DoS CVE, both fixed in 3.4.0, but this app's actual JWT usage pattern avoids the specific exploit path

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** python-jose==3.3.0 carries a critical (CVSS 9.3) algorithm-confusion CVE and a JWT/JWE-bomb DoS CVE, both fixed in 3.4.0, but this app's actual JWT usage pattern avoids the specific exploit path
- **Description:** OSV confirms python-jose 3.3.0 is listed for GHSA-6c5p-j8vq-pqhj/CVE-2024-33663 ('algorithm confusion with OpenSSH ECDSA keys and other key formats', CVSS 9.3, fixed in 3.4.0) and GHSA-cjwg-qfpm-7377/'JWT bomb' JWE decompression DoS (fixed 3.4.0) -- cross-checked directly against the GitHub Security Advisory pages, which confirm both. Upstream python-jose had no releases between 3.3.0 (2021) and 3.4.0 (which finally shipped the fix), i.e. it sat unpatched/dormant for roughly three years -- worth treating as a low-maintenance dependency going forward. Read backend/app/auth/security.py: `jwt.encode(payload, settings.jwt_secret_key, algorithm=settings.jwt_algorithm)` and `jwt.decode(token, settings.jwt_secret_key, algorithms=[settings.jwt_algorithm])`, with backend/app/core/config.py hardcoding `jwt_algorithm: str = "HS256"`. The decode call passes a single, server-controlled algorithm allowlist derived from settings (not from the incoming token's own header), and no JWE (encryption) is used anywhere in the codebase for tokens -- only JWS. That specific usage pattern is not the vulnerable path either advisory describes (alg-confusion requires the verifier to accept multiple/asymmetric algorithms; the JWE-bomb requires decoding a JWE). So this app's JWT auth is not exploitable via these two CVEs today, but it's the authentication trust boundary sitting on an otherwise-vulnerable, effectively-unmaintained library -- any future change (adding RS/ES256 support, or JWE) would need to happen only after upgrading past 3.4.0.
- **Evidence:** OSV query for python-jose==3.3.0 returned GHSA-6c5p-j8vq-pqhj/CVE-2024-33663 and GHSA-cjwg-qfpm-7377, both 'fixed in 3.4.0'. GitHub advisory pages fetched directly confirm CVE-2024-33663 is CVSS 9.3 critical, algorithm confusion. backend/app/auth/security.py line 34: `jwt.encode(payload, settings.jwt_secret_key, algorithm=settings.jwt_algorithm)`; line 54: `jwt.decode(token, settings.jwt_secret_key, algorithms=[settings.jwt_algorithm])`. backend/app/core/config.py line 30: `jwt_algorithm: str = "HS256"`.
- **Reproduction:** Read C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\backend\app\auth\security.py and backend\app\core\config.py; query https://api.osv.dev/v1/query with {package:{name:'python-jose',ecosystem:'PyPI'},version:'3.3.0'}

#### 63. lxml==5.3.0 has a confirmed XXE CVE in its default iterparse()/ETCompatXMLParser() config, but the app doesn't call those APIs directly -- risk is confined to whatever feedparser/BeautifulSoup do internally when parsing untrusted RSS feeds

- **Severity:** P4

- **Description:** OSV confirms lxml 5.3.0 matches GHSA-vfmq-68hx-4jfw/CVE-2026-41066: 'Default configuration of iterparse() and ETCompatXMLParser() allows XXE to local files.' This is relevant in principle because backend/app/crawler/sources/rss_news.py uses `feedparser` to ingest external, untrusted RSS/Atom threat-news feeds, and lxml is present in requirements.txt as an accelerator lxml can use internally. grep across backend/app for direct calls to `lxml.etree`, `iterparse(`, `ETCompatXMLParser(`, or `BeautifulSoup(` found zero matches -- the app never calls the specific vulnerable APIs itself. I did not audit feedparser's or BeautifulSoup's internal use of lxml (that's inside their own source, not this app's), so I can't rule out the library internals hitting the vulnerable path; this is flagged as a verified-version/low-confirmed-exposure item, worth a cheap patch-level bump given the untrusted-feed-ingestion role, not an active confirmed exploit.

- **Reproduction:** query OSV for lxml==5.3.0; grep -rn 'iterparse|ETCompatXMLParser' C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\backend\app

#### 64. pip-audit gave a false-negative 'No known vulnerabilities found' inside app-backend-1 for packages independently confirmed vulnerable via direct OSV.dev queries from the same container

- **Severity:** info
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** pip-audit gave a false-negative 'No known vulnerabilities found' inside app-backend-1 for packages independently confirmed vulnerable via direct OSV.dev queries from the same container
- **Description:** Ran `pip-audit --no-deps -r <requirements.txt content>` and separately `pip-audit --no-deps --vulnerability-service osv` inside app-backend-1 against the exact pinned versions (including python-jose==3.3.0 and python-multipart==0.0.9). Both runs completed cleanly and reported 'No known vulnerabilities found' -- with no error, warning, or indication of a lookup failure. Querying api.osv.dev directly, from the exact same container, for the exact same package==version pairs immediately returned the CVEs documented above. This means pip-audit cannot be trusted as a sole signal in this environment/setup -- something about its default data-fetch path (possibly caching, possibly a different index it silently falls back to) is producing false negatives. Anyone relying on 'pip-audit came back clean' as evidence of a healthy dependency set in this container would be misled.
- **Evidence:** docker exec app-backend-1 pip-audit --no-deps -r <requirements.txt> -> 'No known vulnerabilities found'. docker exec app-backend-1 pip-audit --no-deps --vulnerability-service osv -r <subset incl. python-jose==3.3.0, python-multipart==0.0.9> -> 'No known vulnerabilities found'. Same container, direct urllib POST to https://api.osv.dev/v1/query for python-jose==3.3.0 -> returns GHSA-6c5p-j8vq-pqhj immediately; for python-multipart==0.0.9 -> returns GHSA-59g5-xgcq-4qw3 immediately.
- **Reproduction:** docker exec app-backend-1 pip-audit --no-deps --vulnerability-service osv -r requirements.txt; compare against direct POST to https://api.osv.dev/v1/query for the same package/version

#### 65. passlib==1.7.4 is upstream-dormant since 2020 but functions correctly with the pinned bcrypt==4.0.1 in this exact container today -- watch item, not a current bug

- **Severity:** info
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** passlib==1.7.4 is upstream-dormant since 2020 but functions correctly with the pinned bcrypt==4.0.1 in this exact container today -- watch item, not a current bug
- **Description:** passlib is used for password hashing (core auth surface) and has had no upstream release since 2020 -- OSV lists zero current CVEs for 1.7.4, but an unmaintained project won't ship a fix if one is found later. There is a well-known community-documented incompatibility between passlib's bcrypt-backend version detection and bcrypt>=4.0 (bcrypt 4.x removed the `__about__.__version__` attribute passlib's older detection code expects). To check whether this app is actually hitting that, I ran `CryptContext(schemes=['bcrypt']).hash()`/`.verify()` live inside app-backend-1 with the pinned bcrypt==4.0.1: it hashed and verified a test string correctly, with only an unrelated Python-3.13 stdlib `crypt`-module deprecation warning (no bcrypt-detection error/warning). So today's exact pinned combination works. Flagging only so that a future bump of bcrypt (without also touching passlib) doesn't silently reintroduce the known compatibility issue in an unmaintained wrapper.
- **Evidence:** docker exec app-backend-1 python -c "from passlib.context import CryptContext; ctx=CryptContext(schemes=['bcrypt']); h=ctx.hash('test-password-123'); print(len(h)); print(ctx.verify('test-password-123', h))" -> hash length 60, verify True, only a DeprecationWarning about stdlib `crypt`. OSV query for passlib==1.7.4 -> 0 vulns.
- **Reproduction:** docker exec app-backend-1 python -c "from passlib.context import CryptContext; ctx=CryptContext(schemes=['bcrypt']); h=ctx.hash('x'); print(ctx.verify('x', h))"

#### 66. python-whois==0.9.6 is a sparsely-maintained package (and part of a confusing family of similarly-named forks) used to resolve analyst-supplied domain strings -- 0 known CVEs, but worth a manual injection-handling check separate from version pinning

- **Severity:** info
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** python-whois==0.9.6 is a sparsely-maintained package (and part of a confusing family of similarly-named forks) used to resolve analyst-supplied domain strings -- 0 known CVEs, but worth a manual injection-handling check separate from version pinning
- **Description:** OSV lists 0 vulnerabilities for python-whois 0.9.6, so this is not a 'known CVE' finding. It's flagged because: (1) there are multiple similarly-named, differently-maintained PyPI packages in this space (`whois`, `pythonwhois`, `python-whois`), which is a mild risk if requirements.txt is ever hand-edited later (typo -> wrong/unmaintained package); (2) it's confirmed used in backend/app/providers/whois_rdap.py to run WHOIS queries against analyst-supplied domain/IOC strings, and WHOIS is a raw text protocol over a socket -- if the domain string isn't validated before being placed in the query, a crafted value containing CRLF could inject additional WHOIS-protocol content to the upstream server. That's an app-code question (not this dependency-inventory dimension's scope to prove), noted here as a recommended follow-up rather than a demonstrated finding.
- **Evidence:** OSV query for python-whois==0.9.6 -> 0 vulns. grep -rln 'python_whois|whois\.' backend/app -> backend/app/providers/whois_rdap.py, backend/app/tests/unit/test_whois_rdap.py.
- **Reproduction:** grep -rn 'whois' C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\backend\app\providers\whois_rdap.py

#### 67. npm audit flags transitive nanoid (<3.3.18, high, DoS on size=0) and postcss (<=8.5.22, high, XSS/arbitrary-file-read via sourceMappingURL) -- both are build-time-only in this app, so the 'high' severity label overstates real runtime risk here

- **Severity:** info
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** npm audit flags transitive nanoid (<3.3.18, high, DoS on size=0) and postcss (<=8.5.22, high, XSS/arbitrary-file-read via sourceMappingURL) -- both are build-time-only in this app, so the 'high' severity label overstates real runtime risk here
- **Description:** `npm audit --omit=dev` in frontend/ additionally flagged nanoid (a transitive dependency, likely pulled in by Next.js tooling) for a DoS that triggers only when a custom generator is called with size=0 -- this app doesn't call nanoid directly, so it would require a caller upstream in the dependency chain to do so, which is outside app control. It also flagged postcss (transitive via Next.js/Tailwind's build pipeline) for CSS-stringify XSS and sourceMappingURL-based arbitrary local-file-disclosure -- but postcss in this app only ever processes the app's own committed Tailwind/CSS source at build time (`next build`), never attacker-supplied CSS at request time, and its output is a static compiled stylesheet served to browsers. Per the task's instruction not to blindly recommend upgrading everything: both are correctly low-priority here despite npm audit's 'high' label, because neither package touches untrusted input in this app's actual runtime path.
- **Evidence:** npm audit --omit=dev in frontend/ output: 'nanoid <3.3.18, Severity: high... custom generators can loop indefinitely when size is zero' and 'postcss <=8.5.22, Severity: high... XSS via Unescaped </style>... Arbitrary file read... via attacker-controlled sourceMappingURL'. Both listed as transitive (node_modules/nanoid, node_modules/next/node_modules/postcss), not direct package.json entries.
- **Reproduction:** cd C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\frontend && npm audit --omit=dev

#### 68. Both manifests are fully version-pinned (no unpinned or range-specified entries found) -- good for reproducibility, but means nothing here auto-receives security patches

- **Severity:** info
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** Both manifests are fully version-pinned (no unpinned or range-specified entries found) -- good for reproducibility, but means nothing here auto-receives security patches
- **Description:** Every line in backend/requirements.txt uses an exact `==` pin (32/32 entries). Every entry in frontend/package.json's dependencies and devDependencies (23/23 entries) is an exact version string with no `^`/`~`/range prefix. Confirmed the installed copy at 'C:\Program Files\IOC Intelligence Platform\app' matches the dev-tree manifests exactly, and that app-backend-1's live `pip list` matches requirements.txt exactly -- there is no drift between declared and running versions. This is good practice (reproducible builds, no surprise transitive bumps) and is precisely why the OSV lookups above could be run against exact, known versions rather than ranges. The tradeoff, as reflected in every finding above, is that none of these will move forward without someone deliberately re-pinning -- recommend a periodic (e.g. quarterly) dependency-refresh pass using `pip-audit --vulnerability-service osv` (not the default service, which produced false negatives here -- see separate finding) and `npm audit`.
- **Evidence:** Read of backend/requirements.txt: all 32 lines contain '=='. Read of frontend/package.json: all 14 dependencies + 9 devDependencies entries are bare exact-version strings. `powershell Get-Content` on the installed copy's requirements.txt and package.json produced byte-identical output to the dev-tree files. docker exec app-backend-1 pip list matches requirements.txt exactly.
- **Reproduction:** Read C:\Users\User\ioc-intel-platform\backend\requirements.txt and frontend\package.json; diff against 'C:\Program Files\IOC Intelligence Platform\app' copies

#### 69. Remaining direct dependencies checked individually via OSV.dev show zero known vulnerabilities at their exact pinned versions

- **Severity:** info
- **Dimension:** Dependency security inventory (backend/requirements.txt + frontend/package.json)
- **Title:** Remaining direct dependencies checked individually via OSV.dev show zero known vulnerabilities at their exact pinned versions
- **Description:** For completeness, ran direct OSV.dev queries for the exact pinned versions of: fastapi 0.115.0, bcrypt 4.0.1, boto3 1.35.24, reportlab 4.2.5 (note: reportlab's historical CVE-2023-33733 RCE via crafted color strings reaching rl_safe_eval affects <3.6.13 -- 4.2.5 postdates that fix by multiple majors), feedparser 6.0.11, react 18.3.1, react-dom 18.3.1, zustand 4.5.5, recharts 2.12.7, react-force-graph-2d 1.25.4, tailwindcss 3.4.12, and eslint 8.57.1 -- all returned 0 vulns. eslint and tailwindcss are one major version behind current (ESLint 9, Tailwind 4) but are devDependencies / build-time-only tooling that never runs in the shipped standalone Next.js output or the backend container, so per the task's instruction against blanket upgrade recommendations, these are correctly deprioritized regardless of major-version gap.
- **Evidence:** Direct OSV.dev queries (api.osv.dev/v1/query) for each package/version pair listed above all returned '-> vulns found: 0'.
- **Reproduction:** For any package, POST {"package":{"name":"<pkg>","ecosystem":"PyPI"|"npm"},"version":"<version>"} to https://api.osv.dev/v1/query

#### 70. structlog is configured but never used — JSON structured logging exists in main.py config only, all actual log output is unstructured plaintext from stdlib logging

- **Severity:** P2
- **Dimension:** Observability review — IOC Intelligence Platform backend (app-backend-1)
- **Title:** structlog is configured but never used — JSON structured logging exists in main.py config only, all actual log output is unstructured plaintext from stdlib logging
- **Description:** `app/main.py` calls `structlog.configure(processors=[structlog.processors.JSONRenderer()])`, suggesting structured JSON logs are intended. But `grep -rln 'structlog' /app/app` shows `structlog` is imported/configured only in `main.py` itself — no other file in the codebase (`grep -c 'logger = logging.getLogger' → 21 files` all use the stdlib `logging` module directly) ever calls `structlog.get_logger()`. The structlog configuration is dead code; every real log line observed is plain unstructured text via `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)` (which also uses Python's default bare format with no timestamp of its own — the only timestamp visible is the one Docker itself stamps on capture via `docker logs -t`, not one the app emits).
- **Evidence:** `app/main.py` line 26: `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)` followed by line 27: `structlog.configure(processors=[structlog.processors.JSONRenderer()])`. Every observed log line across all three test operations was plaintext, e.g. `INFO:httpx:HTTP Request: GET https://api.abuseipdb.com/... "HTTP/1.1 401 Unauthorized"` and `WARNING:app.ai.service:Provider summary generation failed for spamhaus: RuntimeError('Could not reach Ollama at http://host.docker.internal:19999...')` — never JSON, never containing an app-emitted timestamp, level-as-field, or any structured key/value that a log aggregator (e.g. an ELK/Loki pipeline) could parse and filter on. This means any real log-aggregation tooling stood up in front of `docker logs` would have to fall back to fragile plaintext regex parsing rather than structured field queries.
- **Reproduction:** 1. `docker exec app-backend-1 grep -rln structlog /app/app` → only `app/main.py`. 2. `docker logs -t app-backend-1 --tail 50` → confirm every line is stdlib plaintext, not JSON.

#### 71. High-volume routine polling (`POST /api/v1/runtime/ai-active`) floods the log stream, burying security-relevant single-line events in noise

- **Severity:** P2
- **Dimension:** Observability review — IOC Intelligence Platform backend (app-backend-1)
- **Title:** High-volume routine polling (`POST /api/v1/runtime/ai-active`) floods the log stream, burying security-relevant single-line events in noise
- **Description:** The frontend appears to poll `POST /api/v1/runtime/ai-active` on a very tight interval (roughly every 250-300ms based on observed timestamps), and each poll produces its own uvicorn access-log line indistinguishable in format/verbosity from a security-relevant event like a failed login. With no severity/category distinction beyond the URL path and no correlation ID, a human or simple `grep`/tail-watching operator has to visually scan through dozens of near-identical routine polling lines to spot the one login-failure or lookup line they care about.
- **Evidence:** `docker logs -t app-backend-1 --tail 60` captured immediately after the failed-login test contains 40+ lines of `INFO:     127.0.0.1:38070 - "POST /api/v1/runtime/ai-active HTTP/1.1" 200 OK` in a ~15-second window (10:43:27.517 through 10:43:45.377, roughly one every 275ms from the same client:port), with the single `"POST /api/v1/auth/login HTTP/1.1" 401 Unauthorized` line I was looking for sandwiched in the middle at 10:43:30.720 — visually indistinguishable in format/color/level from the noise around it (all `INFO`).
- **Reproduction:** 1. Load/leave the frontend open against the live backend for ~15 seconds. 2. `docker logs -t app-backend-1 --tail 60` — count the `runtime/ai-active` lines vs. any single security-relevant event in the same window.

#### 72. Prometheus metrics exist and are useful for aggregate trends, but provide no per-incident detail (no correlation with logs, no per-user/IOC labels)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Observability review — IOC Intelligence Platform backend (app-backend-1)
- **Title:** Prometheus metrics exist and are useful for aggregate trends, but provide no per-incident detail (no correlation with logs, no per-user/IOC labels)
- **Description:** This is a partial mitigant worth noting alongside the findings above, not a standalone defect: `prometheus_fastapi_instrumentator`'s `/metrics` endpoint is live and does track `http_requests_total{handler=...,method=...,status=...}` counters, which would let an operator notice e.g. an elevated 4xx rate on `/api/v1/auth/login` or `/api/v1/lookup/stream` on a dashboard. But these are pure aggregate counters — no exemplar linking a specific metric increment back to a specific log line, trace, user, or request, and no latency histogram was observed being scraped in this quick check either (labels are only method/status/handler). This narrows, but does not close, the gap identified above: it can tell you THAT something anomalous is trending, never WHICH specific requests/users were involved, which is what an incident investigation actually needs.
- **Evidence:** `curl http://localhost:8000/metrics` returned real counters, e.g.:
`http_requests_total{handler="/api/v1/lookup/stream",method="POST",status="4xx"} 8.0`
`http_requests_total{handler="/api/v1/auth/login",method="POST",status="2xx"} 5.0`
— confirming metrics infrastructure is present and functioning, but with no per-request or per-actor granularity.
- **Reproduction:** `curl http://localhost:8000/metrics | grep http_requests_total` — observe labels are limited to handler/method/status, nothing more granular.

#### 73. /providers renders its full tab UI for a non-admin (analyst) role but shows a blank content area with no access-denied message when the backing APIs correctly 403

- **Severity:** P2
- **Dimension:** Fresh-eyes documentation validation (docs/QUICKSTART.md, docs/USER_GUIDE.md, docs/ADMIN_GUIDE.md only, plus documentation/DOCUMENTATION_SOURCE/*.md chapters as needed)
- **Title:** /providers renders its full tab UI for a non-admin (analyst) role but shows a blank content area with no access-denied message when the backing APIs correctly 403
- **Description:** An analyst-role session can load http://localhost:3000/providers and see the page header, description text, and all four tab buttons ('AI Providers', 'IOC Providers', 'Audit Log', 'Network Access'); clicking any tab produces a completely empty content area with zero error text, toast, or 'access denied' messaging. Network capture confirms the backend correctly returns 403 for GET /api/v1/runtime/ai-providers, /runtime/ioc-providers, and /runtime/audit-log for an analyst JWT (so this is not an RBAC/security bug), but the frontend gives no feedback at all, and none of the three docs mention that this page exists in a role-gated, silently-empty state for non-admins.
- **Evidence:** Captured browser console/network log: '403 GET http://localhost:8000/api/v1/runtime/ai-providers', '403 GET http://localhost:8000/api/v1/runtime/audit-log?limit=50', '403 GET http://localhost:8000/api/v1/runtime/ioc-providers' for a JWT with role:'analyst'; page innerText for the tab area was empty (just the header/description/tab labels).
- **Reproduction:** Log in as an analyst-role user. Navigate to /providers. Observe page chrome renders normally but the content area under any tab is blank. Open the network tab: 403 GET http://localhost:8000/api/v1/runtime/ai-providers / /runtime/ioc-providers / /runtime/audit-log.
- **category:** ux/documentation-gap

#### 74. USER_GUIDE.md's SSE-sequence notation reads as batched (all results, then all summaries) but the real stream interleaves them per-provider, matching QUICKSTART's diagram rather than USER_GUIDE's prose

- **Severity:** P3
- **Dimension:** Fresh-eyes documentation validation (docs/QUICKSTART.md, docs/USER_GUIDE.md, docs/ADMIN_GUIDE.md only, plus documentation/DOCUMENTATION_SOURCE/*.md chapters as needed)
- **Title:** USER_GUIDE.md's SSE-sequence notation reads as batched (all results, then all summaries) but the real stream interleaves them per-provider, matching QUICKSTART's diagram rather than USER_GUIDE's prose
- **Description:** USER_GUIDE.md states the order as 'detected → N × provider_result → N × provider_summary → correlation → final_assessment → done', which read literally implies all provider_result events arrive before any provider_summary events. The actual captured event log interleaves them per provider (a provider's provider_result is immediately followed by that same provider's provider_summary, before the next provider's result appears), consistent with QUICKSTART.md's own mermaid loop diagram but not with USER_GUIDE's flatter notation. Not a functional defect, just an internal documentation-clarity inconsistency between the two guides that could mislead someone parsing the event order literally.
- **Evidence:** Captured live event log for a 127.0.0.1 lookup: 'provider_result: spamhaus (ok)' -> 'provider_summary: spamhaus' -> 'provider_result: internet_intelligence (ok)' -> 'provider_summary: internet_intelligence' -> 'provider_result: threatfox (not_configured)' -> ... -> 'correlation' -> 'final_assessment' -> 'done' -- i.e. per-provider pairing, not two separate batched phases.
- **Reproduction:** Run any lookup and capture the debug 'Event log' panel or raw SSE stream.
- **category:** documentation-clarity
